in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{j})}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\lhd_{\sigma_{j}}}}\mathcal{M}_{j}
producing exclusively letters in
X
such that, for some
a\in X
, the edge
q^{\prime}\xrightarrow{a}(q^{\prime},a)
does not belong to
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{j})}
.

Proof 4.48
.

If this was not the case, the
subgraph
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{j})}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\lhd_{\sigma_{j}}}}\mathcal{M}_{j}
consisting of the vertices that can be reachable from
(q,m)
by reading letters in
X
would form an
X
-Adam-closed
subgraph.

Lemma 4.49
.

For each label
\Sigma_{i}
of the
children
of the
root
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}
admits some
\Sigma_{i}
-closed subgame
contained in a
\Sigma_{i}
-region of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{D}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}}}
.

Proof 4.50
.

Assume that the
full attractor decomposition
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}
induced by
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{D}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}}}
is the following:

V=S_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup S_{r}\;\sqcup\;A_{1}\sqcup\dots A_{r}\;\sqcup\;B_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup B_{s},

We fix the following
strategy

\mathsf{strat}
for
Eve
in the
letter game
:

•

whenever the play lands to
B_{j}
, where
B_{j}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Attr}_{\mathcal{G}_{j}^{\prime}}(x)}
for some even colour
x
, she forces producing colour
x
,

•

whenever the play arrives to some
A_{j}
, she forces going to
S_{j}
,

•

in regions
S_{j}
she uses the
strategy

(\mathcal{M}_{j},\sigma_{j})
. More precisely, let
m_{v}
be the state of
\mathcal{M}_{j}
such that
(\mathcal{M}_{j,v},\sigma_{j})

implements
a
winning strategy
for
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}(V_{j})}
from
(v,m_{v})
. Each time that the play arrives to a vertex
v
in
V_{j}
from a different region,
Eve
uses
(\mathcal{M}_{j,v},\sigma_{j})
.

Claim 3
.

Let
\rho
be a
play
consistent with
\mathsf{strat}
(from any vertex), and let
y\geq-1
be the maximal odd number such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(\rho)
is contained in a
y
-avoiding region

\mathcal{S}
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{D}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}}}
. Then, either
\rho
eventually stays in a
\Sigma_{i}
-region

S_{j}
contained in
\mathcal{S}
, or the minimal colour produced infinitely often by
\rho
is
y+1
.

{subproof}

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Attr}_{\mathcal{S}}(y+1)}\sqcup V_{1}\sqcup A_{1}\sqcup\dots\dots,V_{l}\sqcup A_{l}
be the
attractor decomposition
of
\mathcal{S}
appearing in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{D}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}}}
.
By definition of an
attractor decomposition
, each time that the play leaves a
V_{j}
region, the next vertex is in
v^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Attr}_{\mathcal{S}}(y+1)}\sqcup V_{1}\sqcup A_{1}\sqcup\dots V_{j-1}\sqcup A_{j-1}
.
First, if
V_{j}
is a
y+2
-avoiding region
,
\rho
cannot stay in it (by maximality of
y
).
Thus, if
\rho
does not eventually stay in a
\Sigma_{i}
-region
, it leaves regions
V_{j}
infinitely often, so it must produce
y+1
infinitely often too. Since
\mathcal{S}
is a
y
-avoiding region
, no colour smaller than
y+1
is produced.

We obtain as a consequence that
\mathsf{strat}
is
winning for Eve
from any initial position: any
play
staying in a
y
-avoiding region
and producing infinitely many
y+1
’s is winning, and if a
play
eventually stays in a
\Sigma_{i}
-region

S_{j}
, it has to be
winning
since the
strategy

implemented by

(\mathcal{M}_{j},\sigma_{j})
is
winning
in there.

We remark that we can extract a
\Sigma_{i}
-FSCC
from any
\Sigma_{i}
-Adam-closed

subgraph
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{j})}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\lhd_{\sigma_{j}}}}\mathcal{M}_{j}
, that will be contained in the
\Sigma_{i}
-region

S_{j}
, so it suffices to prove the existence of such
\Sigma_{i}
-Adam-closed
subgraphs. We also recall that in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{j})}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\lhd_{\sigma_{j}}}}\mathcal{M}_{j}
all choices are left to Adam, so he can choose to produce any path in this product whenever the play arrives to a vertex
v
in
S_{j}
.

Suppose by contradiction that no
accessible

\Sigma_{i}
-Adam-closed

subgraph
exists in any of the products. We consider a
play
in which
Adam
does the following:

(a)

the letters that he gives form a word
w\in\Sigma^{\omega}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(w)=\Sigma_{i}
,

(b)

each time that the
play
arrives to a region
S_{j}
, he exists this region in a finite number of steps.

Indeed, he can ensure to exit regions
S_{j}
while only producing letters in
\Sigma_{i}
by Lemma
4.47
. By Claim
3
, the minimal colour produced infinitely often by such a play is even.
By Remark
4.38
, we can project such a play in the automaton
\mathcal{A}
, obtaining an
accepting

run over

w
. This is a contradiction, since
w\notin{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}(\mathcal{F})}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}
(because
\Sigma_{i}\notin\mathcal{F}
).
We conclude that some
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{j})}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\lhd_{\sigma_{j}}}}\mathcal{M}_{j}
admits a
\Sigma_{i}
-FSCC
, and therefore
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}
admits some
\Sigma_{i}
-closed subgame
.

We can now infer Proposition
4.28
in the case in which the
root
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is
round
: from Lemma
4.49
, we obtain
\Sigma_{i}
-closed subgames
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{A}}}
for each
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
that are moreover contained in
\Sigma_{i}
-regions
. Therefore, their
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{aut}_{\mathcal{A}}}
-projections are disjoint (Remark
4.37
), and each of these projections
induces
an
HD-subautomaton
recognising
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\left.\kern-1.2pt\mathcal{F}\mathchoice{\vphantom{\big|}}{}{}{}\right|_{\Sigma_{i}}}}
(Lemma
4.46
).

4.3
A minimal history-deterministic Rabin automaton

In this section, we present the construction of a
history-deterministic

Rabin

automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
for a
Muller language

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}(\mathcal{F})}
using the
Zielonka tree

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
, and prove its minimality (Theorem
4.64
).
The automaton
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
can be seen as a quotient of the
ZT-parity-automaton
; that is,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
is obtained by merging some states of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
. Thus, we replace the complexity in the number of states by complexity in the
acceptance condition
.
The size of the
automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
is a well-studied parameter of
Zielonka trees
: its
round-branching width
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})}
. This parameter was introduced by Dziembowski, Jurdziński and Walukiewicz
[
34
]
(under the name of
memory of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
) and shown to coincide with the memory required by
Eve
to
win
in
games
using
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}(\mathcal{F})}
as an
acceptance condition
(see Proposition
4.65
below). In this paper, we are not concerned with the memory of winning conditions, but we will use the result from
[
34
]
to obtain the minimality of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
.

We note that this construction is asymmetric, in the sense that we show it for
Rabin

automata
, but not for
Streett

automata
(their dual notion). The reason why we cannot dualize the construction is due to the semantics of
non-deterministic

automata
. However, we could use the same idea to obtain a minimal
universal

history-deterministic

Streett

automaton
(we refer to
[
12
]
for the definition of universal HD automata).

4.3.1
The Zielonka-tree-HD-Rabin-automaton

Definition 4.51

(
[
34
]
)
.

Let
T
be a
tree
with nodes partitioned into
round
and
square nodes
, and let
T_{1},\dots,T_{k}
be the
subtrees of
T
rooted at
the
children
of the
root
of
T
.
We define inductively the
round-branching width
of
T
, denoted
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{rbw}(T)}
as:

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}(T)}=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }T\text{ has exactly one {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}node},}\\[2.84526pt]
\max\{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}(T_{1})},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}(T_{k})}\}&\text{ if the {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}root} is {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}square},}\\[2.84526pt]
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{k}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}(T_{i})}&\text{ if the {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}root} is {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}round}.}\end{cases}

The next lemma directly follows from the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}(T)}
.

Lemma 4.52
.

Let
T=(N=N_{\bigcirc}\sqcup N_{\Box},\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}})
be a
tree
with nodes partitioned into
round
and
square
nodes.
There exists a mapping
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}(T)\to\{1,2,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}(T)}\}
satisfying:

\displaystyle\text{If }n\in N\text{ is a {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}round node} with children }n_{1}\neq n_{2}\text{, for any pair}

\displaystyle\text{of leaves }l_{1}\text{ and }l_{2}\text{ below }n_{1}\text{ and }n_{2}\text{, respectively, }{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}(l_{1})\neq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}(l_{2}).

(
\star
)

Example 4.53
.

Let
\mathcal{F}=\{\{a,b\},\{a,c\},\{b\}\}
be the family of subsets considered in Example
4.5
.
The
round-branching width
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
is
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})}=2
. A labelling
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\{1,2\}
satisfying Property
4.52
is given by
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}(\theta)={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}(\xi)=1
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}(\zeta)=2
.
This labelling is represented in the
Zielonka tree

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
on the left of Figure
14
.

Definition 4.54

(Zielonka-tree-HD-Rabin-automaton)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}2^{\Sigma}_{+}}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}=(N={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{\bigcirc}}\sqcup{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{\Box}},\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}})
be its
Zielonka tree
and
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\eta}{}{}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\{1,2,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})}\}
be a mapping satisfying Property (
4.52
).
We define the
ZT-HD-Rabin-automaton

\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}=(Q,\Sigma,I,\Gamma,\Delta,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Rabin}_{\Gamma}(R)})
as a (non-deterministic)
automaton
using a
Rabin

acceptance condition
, where:

•

Q=\{1,2,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})}\}
,

•

I=Q
,
8
8

8

Any non-empty subset of
Q
can be chosen as the set of
initial states
.

•

\Gamma=N
(the colours of the
acceptance condition
are the nodes of the
Zielonka tree
),

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\delta}(q,a)=\{\big({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Jump}}(l,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(l,a)),{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(l,a)\big)\mid l\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\text{ such that }{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}(l)=q\}
,

•

R=\{(G_{n},R_{n})\}_{n\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{\bigcirc}}}
, where
G_{n}
and
R_{n}
are defined as follows: Let
n
be a
round node
and
n^{\prime}
be any node of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
,

\begin{cases}n^{\prime}\in G_{n}&\text{ if }n^{\prime}=n,\\
n^{\prime}\in R_{n}&\text{ if }n^{\prime}\neq n\text{ and }n\text{ is not an {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}ancestor} of }n^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Remark 4.55
.

Although we will usually say that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
is
the

ZT-HD-Rabin-automaton
of
\mathcal{F}
, the structure of this automaton is not unique, it depends on two choices: the
order
over the nodes of the
Zielonka tree
and the mapping
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}
.

The intuition behind this definition is the following. The
automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
has
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})}
states, and each of them can be associated to a subset of
leaves
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
by
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}^{-1}(q)
. The mapping
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}
is such that the lowest common ancestor of two
leaves
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}^{-1}(q)
is a
square node
.
As for the
ZT-parity-automaton
, for each
leaf
of
l\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})
and letter
a\in\Sigma
, we identify the
deepest
ancestor
n={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(l,a)
containing
a
in its label, and, using the
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Jump}}
function, pick a
leaf

l^{\prime}
below the next
child
of
n
. We add a transition
q\xrightarrow{a:n}q^{\prime}
if there are
leaves

l\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}^{-1}(q)
and
l^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}^{-1}(q^{\prime})
giving such a path (we note that the output colour is given by
n={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(l,a)
, although this node does not appear as a state of the automaton). This way, we can identify a
run
in the
automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
with a promenade through the nodes of the
Zielonka tree
in which jumps between
leaves
with the same
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}
-image are allowed. If during this promenade a unique minimal node (for
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}}
) is visited infinitely often, it is not difficult to see that the sequence of input colours belongs to
\mathcal{F}
if and only if the label of this minimal node belongs to
\mathcal{F}
(it is a
round node
). The
Rabin condition
over the set of nodes of the
Zielonka tree
is devised so that it accepts exactly these sequences of nodes (see Lemma
4.59
below).

Another way of presenting the
automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
is as a quotient of the
deterministic

parity

automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
. Indeed, the graph structure and the
labelling
by
input letters
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
is obtained by merging the states of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
(which are the
leaves
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
) with the same
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}
-image, and keeping all the transitions between them. However, a
parity

acceptance condition
over this smaller structure is no longer sufficient to accept
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}(\mathcal{F})}
.

Example 4.56
.

The
ZT-HD-Rabin-automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
of the family
\mathcal{F}=\{\{a,b\},\{a,c\},\{b\}\}
from Example
4.5
is shown on the right of Figure
14
.
The
Zielonka tree

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
appears on the left of the figure, and the labelling
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})\to\{1,2\}
is represented by the numbers below its
branches
.

The
Rabin

condition
of this automaton is given by two
Rabin pairs
(corresponding to the
round nodes
of the
Zielonka tree
):

G_{\beta}=\{\beta\}
,

R_{\beta}=\{\alpha,\lambda,\xi,\zeta\}
,

G_{\lambda}=\{\lambda\}
,

R_{\lambda}=\{\alpha,\beta,\theta\}
.

We note that the automaton
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
is obtained by merging the states
\theta
and
\xi
from the
ZT-parity-automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
appearing in Figure
10
, and replacing the
output colours
by suitable nodes from the
Zielonka tree
.

Figure 14
:
On the left, the
Zielonka tree
of
\mathcal{F}=\{\{a,b\},\{a,c\},\{b\}\}
. On the right, the
ZT-HD-Rabin- automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
. Blue transitions correspond to those coming from
leaf

\theta
, and green ones to those originating from
leaf

\xi
.

Remark 4.57
.

We observe that the
automaton
from Figure
14
presents
duplicated edges
, in the sense that there are two transitions
q\xrightarrow{a:x}q^{\prime}
and
q\xrightarrow{a:y}q^{\prime}
between the same pair of states and reading the same
input letter
.
We can always avoid this and remove
duplicated edges
from any
automaton
. We provide a proof in Appendix
D
(Proposition
D.1
). For the language from the previous example, an equivalent automaton is proposed in Figure
19

Correctness of the Zielonka-tree-HD-Rabin-automaton.

Proposition 4.58

(Correctness)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}2^{\Sigma}_{+}}
be a family of non-empty subsets. Then,

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{F})}.

Moreover, the
automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
is
history-deterministic
.

Lemma 4.59
.

Let
u=n_{0}n_{1}n_{2}\dots\in N^{\omega}
be an infinite sequence of nodes of the
Zielonka tree
. The word
u
belongs to
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Rabin}_{N}(R)}
, for
R=\{(G_{n},R_{n})\}_{n\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{\bigcirc}}}
the Rabin condition of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
, if and only if there is a unique minimal node for the
ancestor relation
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)
and this minimal node is
round
(recall that the root is the minimal element for
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}}
).

Proof 4.60
.

Assume that there is a unique minimal node in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)
, called
n
, and that
n
is
round
. We claim that
u
is
accepted by the Rabin pair

(G_{n},R_{n})
. It is clear that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)\cap G_{n}\neq\emptyset
, because
n\in G_{n}
. It suffices to show that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)\cap R_{n}=\emptyset
: By minimality, any other node
n^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)
is a
descendant
of
n
(equivalently,
n
is an
ancestor
of
n^{\prime}
), so
n^{\prime}\notin R_{n}
.

Conversely, assume that
u\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Rabin}_{N}(R)}
. Then, there is some
round node

n\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{\bigcirc}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)\cap G_{n}\neq\emptyset
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)\cap R_{n}=\emptyset
. Since
G_{n}=\{n\}
, we deduce that
n\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)
. Moreover, as
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)\cap R_{n}=\emptyset
, all nodes in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)
are descendants of
n
. We conclude that
n
is the unique minimal node in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)
, and it is
round
.

Lemma 4.61
.

There exists a
morphism

of automata

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
.

Proof 4.62
.

We define the morphism
\varphi
as follows:

•

\varphi_{V}(l)={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}(l)
, for
l\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})
,

•

for a transition
e=l\xrightarrow{a:c}l^{\prime}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
, we let
\varphi_{E}(e)=({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}(l),a,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(l,a),l^{\prime})
.

It is clear that
\varphi
is a
weak morphism
. We prove that it
preserve the acceptance of runs
. Let
\rho=l_{0}\xrightarrow{w_{0}}l_{1}\xrightarrow{w_{1}}l_{2}\xrightarrow{w_{2}}\dots\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Run}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}
be an infinite run in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
(the only
run over

w_{0}w_{1}w_{2}\dots\in\Sigma^{\omega}
), and let
n_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(l_{i},w_{i})
. By definition of the morphism, the
output
of the
run

\rho^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\varphi}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\rho)
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
is
\gamma^{\prime}(\rho^{\prime})=n_{0}n_{1}n_{2}\dots\in N^{\omega}
. In the proof of Proposition
4.10
, we proved (Claims
1
and
2
) that there exists a unique node
n_{w}
appearing infinitely often in
\gamma^{\prime}(\rho^{\prime})
. Moreover, we proved that
\rho
is
accepting
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
if and only if
n_{w}
is
round
. Lemma
4.59
allows us to conclude that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\varphi}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\rho)
is
accepting
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
if and only if
\rho
is
accepting
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
.

Proof 4.63

(Proof of Proposition
4.58
)
.

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{F})}
:
Let
w\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}
and let
u\in N^{\omega}
be the sequence of nodes produced as
output
of an
accepting

run over

w
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
. By Lemma
4.59
, there is a unique minimal node
n
for
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}}
appearing infinitely often in
u
and moreover
n
is
round
.
Let
n_{1},\dots,n_{k}
be an enumeration of the
children
of
n
(
from left to right
), with labels
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{i})\subseteq\Sigma
(we remark that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{i})\notin\mathcal{F}
, for
1\leq i\leq k
). We will prove that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(w)\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n)
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(w)\nsubseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq k
. By definition of the
Zielonka tree
, as
n
is
round
, this implies that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(w)\in\mathcal{F}
.

Since eventually all nodes produced as
output
are
descendants
of
n
(by minimality),
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(w)
must be contained in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n)
(by definition of the transitions of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
).

We suppose, towards a contradiction, that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(w)\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{j})
for some
1\leq j\leq k
.
Let
Q_{i}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}(l)\>:\>l\text{ is a {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}leaf} {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}below} }n_{i}\}
be the set of states corresponding to
leaves
under
n_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq k
. We can assume that the
leaves
corresponding to transitions of an accepting
run over

w
are all
below

n
, and therefore, transitions of such a run only visit states in
\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}Q_{i}
. Indeed, eventually this is going to be the case, because if some leaves
l,l^{\prime}
corresponding to a transition
(q,a,n^{\prime},q^{\prime})
are not below
n
, then
n^{\prime}
would not be a
descendant
of
n
(since
n^{\prime}
is the least common ancestor of
l
and
l^{\prime}
).
Also, by Property (
4.52
), we have
Q_{i}\cap Q_{j}=\emptyset
, for all
i\neq j
.
By definition of the transitions of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
, if
a\in\Sigma
is a letter in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n)
but not in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{i})
, all transitions from some state in
Q_{i}
reading the colour
a
go to
Q_{i+1}
, for
1\leq i\leq k-1
(and to
Q_{1}
if
i=k
).
Also, if
a\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{i})
, transitions from states in
Q_{i}
reading
a
stay in
Q_{i}
. We deduce that a run over
w
will eventually only visit states in
Q_{j}
, for some
j
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(w)\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{j})
. However, the only transitions
from
Q_{j}
that would produce
n
as output are those corresponding to a colour
a\notin{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{j})
, so the node
n
is not produced infinitely often, a contradiction.

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{F})}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}
and
history-determinism
: We claim that the existence of a
morphism

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
(Lemma
4.61
) and the correctness of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
(Proposition
4.10
) imply that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}(\mathcal{F})}
. Indeed, if
\rho
is an
accepting run
over
w\in\Sigma^{\omega}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\varphi}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\rho)
is an
accepting run
over
w
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
.
We can moreover use
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
and
\varphi
to define a
sound

resolver

(r_{0},r)
for
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
: we let
r_{0}=\varphi(q_{0})
be the image of the
initial state
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
. If
\rho_{R}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Run}^{\mathsf{fin}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}
is the image under
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\varphi}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}
of some
finite run

\rho_{P}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Run}^{\mathsf{fin}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}
, we let
r(\rho_{R},a)=\varphi(e)
, where
e
is the only
a
-labelled transition from
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Target}}(\rho_{P})
. We define
r
arbitrarily in other case. This way, for every
w\in\Sigma^{\omega}
, the
run induced by

r
over
w
is the image of a
run over

w
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
, which must be
accepting
if
w\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}(\mathcal{F})}
.

4.3.2
Optimality of the Zielonka-tree-HD-Rabin-automaton

We devote this section to the proof of the optimality of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
.

Theorem 4.64

(Optimality of the
ZT-HD-Rabin-automaton
)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
history-deterministic

Rabin

automaton

accepting
a
Muller language

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{F})}
. Then,
|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}|\leq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}|\mathcal{A}|}
.

Proposition 4.65

(
[
34
]
)
.

Let
L={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{F})}
be a
Muller language
.

1.

If
Eve

wins
a
game
with
L
as
acceptance set
from a position
v
, there is a
winning strategy from

v
for her
implemented by
a
memory structure
of size
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})}
.

2.

There exists a
game

\mathcal{G}
using
L
as
acceptance condition
in which
Eve
can win from a position
v
, but there is no
winning strategy from

v
for her
implemented by
a
memory structure
of size strictly smaller than
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})}
.

Lemma 4.66

(
[
51
,
94
]
)
.

Rabin languages
are positionally determined, that is, if
Eve

wins
a
game
using a
Rabin

acceptance condition
from a position
v
, there is a
winning strategy from

v
for her
implemented by
a
memory structure
of size
1
.

Corollary 4.67
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
history-deterministic

Rabin

automaton
. Then, if
Eve

wins
a
game
with
\mathbb{W}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}
as
acceptance set
from a position
v
, there is a
winning strategy from

v
for her
implemented by
a
memory structure
of size
|\mathcal{A}|
.

Proof 4.68
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a
game
with
\mathbb{W}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}
as
acceptance set
. In order to be able to take the
product
by
\mathcal{A}
and obtain an equivalent game, we transform
\mathcal{G}
into a
game

suitable for transformations
. Let
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
be the game obtained from
\mathcal{G}
in the following way: for every edge
e=v\xrightarrow{a}v^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{G}
, we add a position
(v,e)
controlled by
Eve
and replace edge
e
by
v\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}(v,e)\xrightarrow{a}v^{\prime}
. It is clear that
Eve
wins
\mathcal{G}
from a vertex
v
if and only if she
wins

\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
from that same vertex.
By Proposition
2.7
, if
Eve

wins

\mathcal{G}
from a vertex
v
, she
wins

\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ltimes}}\mathcal{A}
from a vertex
(v,q_{0})
, where
q_{0}
is an initial vertex of
\mathcal{A}
. Moreover, the
game

\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ltimes}}\mathcal{A}
uses the
acceptance set
from
\mathcal{A}
, which is a
Rabin language
, so, by Lemma
4.66
, she can win using a
strategy
given by a function
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\sigma}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{Eve}}}\to\tilde{E}
, where
Q
is the set of states of
\mathcal{A}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\tilde{V}_{\mathrm{Eve}}}
the vertices
controlled by

Eve
in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
(a subset of
({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}V_{\mathrm{Eve}}}\sqcup(V\times E))\times Q
).
We build a
memory structure

(\mathcal{M},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\sigma}_{\mathcal{M}})
of size
|Q|
that projects the
strategy

implemented by

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\sigma}
onto
\mathcal{G}
:

•

its set of states is
M=Q
,

•

the initial state is
q_{0}
,

•

the
update function

\mu\colon M\times E\to M
sends
\mu(q,e)=q^{\prime}
if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\sigma}((v,e),q)=((v,e),q)\xrightarrow{}(v^{\prime},q^{\prime})
is the move chosen by
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\sigma}
from vertex
((v,e),q)
,

•

for
v\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}V_{\mathrm{Eve}}}
,
q\in M
, we let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\sigma}_{\mathcal{M}}(v,q)=e
if
e
is the move chosen by
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\sigma}
from
(v,q)
, that is, if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\sigma}(v,q)=(v,q)\xrightarrow{}((v,q),e)
.

Since
\sigma

implements
a
winning strategy
in
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ltimes}}\mathcal{A}
from
(v,q_{0})
, its projection onto
\mathcal{G}
via the
memory structure

(\mathcal{M},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\sigma}_{\mathcal{M}})
is a
strategy
that verifies that any play
consistent with
it produces as
output
a word in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}
, so it is
winning
.

Theorem
4.64
is obtained by combining the fact that
|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}|={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}})}
with Proposition
4.65
(second item) and Corollary
4.67
.

5
The alternating cycle decomposition: An optimal approach to Muller transition systems

In Section
4
, we have provided minimal
parity
and
Rabin

automata
for
Muller languages
, using the
Zielonka tree
.
We can use these
automata
to transform
Muller

transition systems
, by applying the
product construction
.
However, this approach overlooks the structure of the
transition system
, meaning it does not take into account the relevant interplay between the
underlying graph
and the
acceptance condition
.

In this section, we present our main contributions: optimal transformations of
Muller

transition systems
into
parity
and
Rabin
ones.
The key novelty is that they precisely capture the way the
transition system
interacts with the
acceptance condition
.
This is achieved by generalising
Zielonka trees
from
Muller languages
to
Muller

transition systems
; we define the
alternating cycle decomposition
(ACD), consisting in a collection of Zielonka-tree-like structures subsuming all the structural information of the
transition system
necessary to determine whether a
run
is
accepting
or not.
More precisely, the
ACD
is a succinct representation of the
alternating chains of loops
of a
Muller

automaton
, in the sense of Wagner
[
93
]
. The alternating chains of loops of a
DMA
are known to determine the
parity index
of the language it
recognises

[
93
]
, and, as we will show, they also capture the essential information to define optimal transformations of automata.

We start with the definition of the
alternating cycle decomposition
in Section
5.1
. In Section
5.2
, we describe the
ACD-parity-transform
, turning a
DMA

\mathcal{A}
into an equivalent
DPA

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}
. Formally, the validity of this transformation is witnessed by a
locally bijective morphism

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}\to\mathcal{A}
(Proposition
5.19
).
In Section
5.3
, we describe the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
that turns a
DMA

\mathcal{A}
into an equivalent
history-deterministic

Rabin

automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{A})}
. The validity of the transformation is witnessed by an
HD mapping

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{A})}\to\mathcal{A}
(Proposition
5.27
).
These constructions grant strong optimality guarantees. The
automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}
(resp.
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{A})}
) has a minimal number of states amongst
parity
(resp.
Rabin
)
automata
admitting an
HD mapping
to
\mathcal{A}
(Theorems
5.35
and
5.36
). We note that this implies minimality amongst
automata
admitting a
locally bijective morphism
to
\mathcal{A}
.
Moreover, the
acceptance condition
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}
uses an optimal number of
colours
(Theorem
5.34
).
The optimality of these constructions is shown in Section
5.4
. We are able to prove the optimality of both constructions at the same time, by reducing the problem to an application of the minimality of the
ZT-parity-automaton
and the
ZT-HD-Rabin-automaton
.

In all this section, we let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}=({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})
be a
Muller

transition system
with
underlying graph

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}=(V,E,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Source}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Target}},I)
and using a
Muller

acceptance condition

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}=(\gamma,\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{F})})
.

5.1
The alternating cycle decomposition

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\ell_{0}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
be a
cycle
.
We define the
tree of alternating subcycles
of
\ell_{0}
, denoted
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{0})}=(N,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}},\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\nu}{}{}\colon N\to{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})})
as a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
-
labelled tree
with nodes partitioned into
round nodes
and
square nodes
,
N={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{\bigcirc}}\sqcup{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{\Box}}
, such that:

•

The
root
is labelled
\ell_{0}
.

•

If a node is labelled
\ell\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
, and
\ell
is an
accepting cycle
(
\gamma(\ell)\in\mathcal{F}
), then it is a
round node
, and its
children
are labelled exactly with the maximal subcycles
\ell^{\prime}\subseteq\ell
such that
\ell^{\prime}
is
rejecting
(
\gamma(\ell^{\prime})\notin\mathcal{F}
).

•

If a node is labelled
\ell\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
, and
\ell
is a
rejecting cycle
(
\gamma(\ell)\notin\mathcal{F}
), then it is a
square node
, and its
children
are labelled exactly with the maximal subcycles
\ell^{\prime}\subseteq\ell
such that
\ell^{\prime}
is
accepting
(
\gamma(\ell^{\prime})\in\mathcal{F}
).

For a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
-
labelled tree

T=(N,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}\colon N\to{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})})
and
n\in N
, we let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}_{\mathsf{States}}}(n)={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{States}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n))}
be the
set of states
of the
cycle
labelling
n
.

Remark 5.2
.

Let
n
be a node of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{0})}
and let
n_{1}
be a
child
of it. If
\ell^{\prime}
is a
cycle
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{1})\subsetneq\ell^{\prime}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n)
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{1})\text{ is {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}accepting} }\iff\ell^{\prime}\text{ is {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}rejecting} }\iff{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n)\text{ is {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}rejecting}}
.

Definition 5.3

(Alternating cycle decomposition)
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
transition system
, and let
\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\dots,\ell_{k}
be an enumeration of its maximal
cycles
(that is, the edge set of its
SCCs
). We define the
alternating cycle decomposition
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
as the
forest

\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{1})},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{k})}\}
.

We let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}N_{\ell_{i}}}
be the set of nodes of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}
, and
n_{\ell_{i}}
its
root
. We will assume that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{\ell_{i}}}\cap{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{\ell_{j}}}=\emptyset
if
i\neq j
.

We define the
set of nodes of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
to be
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{Nodes}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{k}N_{\ell_{i}}
, and we let
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{Nodes}_{\bigcirc}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}
(resp.
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{Nodes}_{\Box}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}
) be the subset of
round
(resp.
square
) nodes.
As for
Zielonka trees
, from now on we equip the
trees
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
with an arbitrary
order
making them
ordered trees
, without explicitly mentioning it.

We remark that for a
recurrent
vertex
v
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, there is one and only one
tree

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
such that
v\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}_{\mathsf{States}}}(n_{\ell_{i}})
. On the other hand,
transient
vertices do not appear in the trees of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
.

If
v
is a
recurrent
vertex of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, we define the
local subtree at
v
, noted
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
, as the
subtree
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}
containing the nodes

\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}N_{v}}=\{n\in N_{\ell_{i}}\mid v\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}_{\mathsf{States}}}(n)\}
.
If
v
is a
transient
vertex, we define
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
to be a
tree
with a single node.

For
v

recurrent
, as
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{v}}
is a subset of the nodes of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}
, the
tree

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
inherits the
order
from
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}
, as well as its partition into
round
and
square
nodes,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{v}}=N_{v,\bigcirc}{}{}\sqcup N_{v,\Box}{}{}
. Also, it inherits the labelling given by the mapping
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}
, whose restriction to
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
has an image in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
.

Remark 5.4
.

Let
v\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}_{\mathsf{States}}}(n_{\ell_{i}})
. If
n\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{v}}
and
n^{\prime}
is an
ancestor
of
n
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}
, then
n^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{v}}
. In particular,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
is indeed a
subtree
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}
. Also, we note that the
root
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
is
n_{\ell_{i}}
.

For a node
n\in N_{\ell_{i}}
and an edge
e\in\ell_{i}
we define
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{Supp}}(n,e)=n^{\prime}
to be the
deepest

ancestor
of
n
such that
e\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n^{\prime})
. We remark that if
e=v\xrightarrow{}v^{\prime}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(n,e)
is a node in both
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v^{\prime}}}
.

Example 5.5
.

We will use the
transition system

\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
from Figure
15
as a running example.
We have named the edges of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
with letters from
a
to
l
, that are also used as the
output colours
of the
acceptance condition
. The
acceptance set
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
is the
Muller language associated to
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\{\{c,d,e\},\{e\},\{g,h,i\},\{l\},\{h,i,j,k\},\{j,k\}\}.

The
initial vertex
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
,
v_{0}
, is its only
transient vertex
, all the others vertices are
recurrent
.
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
has
2
strongly connected components, corresponding to
cycles

\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
.

Figure 15
:
Transition system

\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
using a
Muller

acceptance condition
given by
\mathcal{F}=\{\{c,d,e\},\{e\},\{g,h,i\},

\{l\},\{h,i,j,k\},\{j,k\}\}
. The two maximal
cycles
,
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
, are encircled by blue and red dashed lines, respectively.

The
alternating cycle decomposition
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
is shown in Figure
16
. It consists of two
trees
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{1})}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{2})}
.
We use Greek letters (in pink) to name the nodes of the tree.
Inside each node we indicate both its label
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n)
and the
set of states
of it. For example,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(\kappa)=\{g,h,i\}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}_{\mathsf{States}}}(\kappa)=\{v_{3},v_{4}\}
.
We have that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(\tau,g)=\kappa
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(\tau,j)=\lambda
.
We highlight in bold orange the
local subtree at
v_{4}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v_{4}}}
. The tree
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v_{0}}}
, consisting in a single node, does not appear in the figure.
The numbering on the right of the trees will be used in the next section.

Figure 16
:
Alternating cycle decomposition
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
. In bold orange, the
local subtree at

v_{4}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v_{4}}}
.

Remark 5.6
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

TS
using as
acceptance set

\mathbb{W}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{F})}
, and let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\overline{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
be the
TS
obtained by replacing
\mathbb{W}
with
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\overline{\mathbb{W}}}=\Gamma^{\omega}\setminus\mathbb{W}
(which is a
Muller language
). Then, the
ACD
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\overline{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
coincides with that of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, with the only difference that the partition into
round
and
square nodes
is inverted:
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}_{\bigcirc}(\mathcal{ACD}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\overline{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}})}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}_{\Box}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}_{\Box}(\mathcal{ACD}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\overline{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}})}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}_{\bigcirc}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}
.

We note that if
\mathcal{A}
is a
DMA

recognising

L\subseteq\Sigma^{\omega}
, the
automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\overline{\mathcal{A}}}
is a
DMA

recognising

\Sigma^{\omega}\setminus L
.

Remark 5.7
.

The
Zielonka tree
can be seen as a special case of the
alternating cycle decomposition
. Indeed, a
Muller language

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{F})}
can be trivially
recognised
by a
DMA

\mathcal{A}
with a single state
q
and self-loops
q\xrightarrow{a:a}q
. The
ACD
of this
automaton
is exactly the
Zielonka tree
of
\mathcal{F}
.

Remark 5.8

(Size and computation of the ACD)
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a Muller
TS
and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
be the
Zielonka tree
of its
acceptance set
. It can be shown that for each vertex
v
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
we have that
|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}|\leq|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}|
, and therefore the size of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is polynomial in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}
.
This, and the question of the complexity of computing the
ACD
is the subject of an independent work
[
26
]
.

Local Muller languages.

For a
recurrent
state
v
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, we define the
local Muller language of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
at
v
as the
Muller language
defined over the alphabet
\Gamma_{v}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
associated to:

\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}=\{\mathcal{C}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\mid\bigcup_{\ell\in\mathcal{C}}\ell\;\text{ is an {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}accepting} {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}cycle}}\}.

We note that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}
is determined by singletons (
\mathcal{C}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}
if and only if
\{\bigcup_{\ell\in\mathcal{C}}\ell\}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}
). For simplicity, and by a slight abuse of notation, we will work as if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
.
Also, to lighten notations, we will just write
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}
to denote
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)})}
whenever no confusion arises.

The following lemma directly follows from the definition of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
and that of the
Zielonka tree
. It provides insight in the structure of the
trees

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
, and it will be a key ingredient in the proof of the optimality of the transformations based on the
alternating cycle decomposition
.

Lemma 5.9
.

The
tree

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}

is the
Zielonka tree
of the family
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}
,
9
9

9

Formally, the labelling
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
goes to
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
, and not to
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}2^{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}}_{+}}
, as required by the definition of the
Zielonka tree
. To obtain a proper
Zielonka tree
with a labelling of nodes
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}^{\prime}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}N_{v}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}2^{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}}_{+}}
, we would have to define
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}^{\prime}(n)=\{\ell^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\mid\ell^{\prime}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n)\}
.
for any recurrent vertex
v
.

5.2
An optimal transformation to parity transition systems

We now define the
ACD-parity-transform
, an optimal transformation turning a
Muller

TS
into a
parity

TS
while preserving
determinism
. In order to obtain the optimality in the number of
output colours
, we need to pay attention to the parity of the minimal colour used in different
SCCs
. To incorporate this parameter in the transformation, we define
positive
and
negative

ACDs
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

transition system
and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{1})},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{k})}\}
be its
alternating cycle decomposition
.

We say that a
tree

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
positive
if
\ell_{i}
is an
accepting cycle
, and that it is
negative
otherwise.
We say that the
alternating cycle decomposition
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
is
positive
if all the
trees
of maximal
height
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
are
positive
, that it is
negative
if all
trees
of maximal
height
are
negative
, and that it is
equidistant
if there are
positive
and
negative
trees of maximal
height
.

As for the
Zielonka tree
, we associate a non-negative integer to each level of the trees of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
via a function
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(n)\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}\to\mathbb{N}
. Let
\ell_{i}
be a maximal
cycle
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
and
n\in N_{\ell_{i}}
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
positive
or
equidistant
:

–

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(n)=0pt(n)
, if
\ell_{i}
is
accepting
,

–

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(n)=0pt(n)+1
, if
\ell_{i}
is
rejecting
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
negative
:

–

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(n)=0pt(n)+2
, if
\ell_{i}
is
accepting
,

–

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(n)=0pt(n)+1
, if
\ell_{i}
is
rejecting
.

We let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
(resp.
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
) be the minimum (resp. maximum) value taken by the function
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}
.

Remark 5.10
.

A node
n
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}
verifies that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(n)
is even if and only if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n)
is an
accepting cycle
(that is, if
n
is a
round node
).

Remark 5.11
.

It is satisfied:

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}=0
if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
positive
or
equidistant
,

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}=1
if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
negative
.

Example 5.12
.

In the previous Example
5.5
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{1})}
is a
positive tree
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{2})}
is
negative
. As
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{2})}
is the tree of maximal
height
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
negative
.
The function
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}
is represented in Figure
16
by the integers on the right of each tree. It takes values
2
and
3
over
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{1})}
(
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(\alpha)=2
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(\beta)=3
), because
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
negative
.
In this example,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}=1
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}=3
.
We note that if we had associated integers
0
and
1
to the levels of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{1})}
, we would have used
4
integers in total, instead of just
3
of them.

Definition 5.13

(ACD-parity-transform)
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

TS
with
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{1})}\allowbreak,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{k})}\}
.
We define the
ACD-parity-transform
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be the
parity

TS

\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}=({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}^{\prime})
, with
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}^{\prime}=(V^{\prime},E^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Source}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Target}}^{\prime},I^{\prime})
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime},\allowbreak[{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}},\allowbreak{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}],{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{parity}})
defined as follows.

Vertices.

The set of vertices is

V^{\prime}=\bigcup_{v\in V}\left(\{v\}\times{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})\right).

Initial vertices.

I^{\prime}=\{(v_{0},n)\mid v_{0}\in I\text{ and }n\text{ is the {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}leftmost} {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}leaf} in }{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v_{0}}}\}
.

Edges and output colours.

For each
(v,n)\in V^{\prime}
and each edge
e=v\xrightarrow{}v^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Out}}(v)
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
we define an edge
e_{n}=(v,n)\xrightarrow{\gamma^{\prime}(e_{n})}(v^{\prime},n^{\prime})
. Formally,

E^{\prime}=\bigcup_{e\in E}\left(\{e\}\times{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Source}}(e)}})\right).

If
v
and
v^{\prime}
are not in the same
SCC
, we let
n^{\prime}
be the
leftmost

leaf
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v^{\prime}}}
and
\gamma^{\prime}(e_{n})={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
.
10
10

10

The colours associated to transitions changing of
SCC
are
almost
arbitrary (we could even leave them
uncoloured
). We define them to be the minimal colour used so that the obtained
transition system
is
normalised
in the sense of Section
6.2
.
If
v
and
v^{\prime}
belong to the same
SCC
, we let:

•

n^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Jump}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v^{\prime}}}}(n,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(n,e))
,

•

\gamma^{\prime}(e_{n})={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(n,e))
.

Labellings.

If
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
is a
labelled transition system
, with labels
l_{V}\colon V\to L_{V}
and
l_{E}\colon E\to L_{E}
, we label
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
by
l^{\prime}_{V^{\prime}}(v,n)=l_{V}(v)
and
l^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}(e_{n})=l_{E}(e)
.

Intuitively, a
run
in the
transition system

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
follows a
run
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
with some extra information, updated in the same manner as it was the case with the
ZT-parity-automaton
.
To define transitions in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
, we move simultaneously in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
and in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
. When
we take a transition
e
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
that goes from
v
to
v^{\prime}
, while being in a node
n
in the
ACD
, we climb the
branch of
n
searching the
lowest
node
\tilde{n}
with
e
and
v^{\prime}
in its label (
\tilde{n}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(n,e)
). We produce as output the
colour corresponding to the level reached. If no such node exists in the current tree (this occurs if we change of
SCC
), we jump to the
root
of the tree containing
v^{\prime}
. After having reached the node
\tilde{n}
, we move to its next child in the tree
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v^{\prime}}}
(in a cyclic way), and we pick the leftmost
leaf
under it.

Example 5.14
.

We show in Figure
17
the
ACD-parity-transform

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
of the
transition system

\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
from Figure
15
(Example
5.5
).
For each vertex
v
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, we make as many copies as
leaves
of the tree
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
. We note that, as
v_{0}
is
transient
, the tree
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v_{0}}}
consists of a single node (by definition), that we name
\iota
.
Transitions are of the form
(e,l)
, for
e
a transition from
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
and
l
a
leaf
of some
local subtree
; these are denoted
e_{l}
in the figure for the sake of space convenience. These labels simply indicate the names of the edges, they should not be interpreted as
input letters
(
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
is not an
automaton
).

We observe that the mappings
\varphi_{V}(v,l)=v
and
\varphi_{E}(e_{l})=e
define a
locally bijective

morphism of transition systems
from
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
to
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
.

Figure 17
:
ACD-parity-transform

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
of the
transition system

\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
from Figure
15
.

Another example can be found in Figure
18
.

Remark 5.15
.

The size of the
ACD-parity-transformation
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
is:

|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}|=\sum_{v\in V}|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})|=\sum_{v\in V_{\mathrm{rec}}}|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})|+|V_{\mathrm{trans}}|,

where
V_{\mathrm{rec}}
and
V_{\mathrm{trans}}
are the sets of
recurrent
and
transient
vertices of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, respectively.

Remark 5.16
.

We remark that if
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}=({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})
is already a
parity

TS
, then the underlying graphs of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
and
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
are isomorphic. In fact, by Proposition
5.19
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
and
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
will also be
isomorphic
as
transition systems
.
In this case, the construction of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
boils down to the application of the procedure described by Carton and Maceiras
[
19
]
.

Remark 5.17
.

The
ACD-parity-transform
is oblivious to the labelling
\gamma
of the
acceptance condition
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
; the only information taken into account to define the graph of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
and its
output colours
is the structure of the
trees
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
.
That is, the definition of this transformation is independent of the actual representation of the
acceptance condition
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
(whether it is Emerson-Lei,
Muller
,
Rabin
…), and we only use that any such representation induces a mapping
f\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\to\{\mathrm{Accept},\mathrm{Reject}\}
.

Remark 5.18
.

The
ZT-parity-automaton
can be seen as a special case of the
ACD-parity-transform
, as
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}}
coincides with the
DPA

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}
, where
\mathcal{A}
is the
DMA
with a single state
recognising

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}(\mathcal{F})}
(see Remark
5.7
).

Correctness of the ACD-parity-transform.

Proposition 5.19

(Correctness of the
ACD-parity-transform
)
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
(labelled)

Muller

TS
and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
be its
ACD-parity-transform
. There is a
locally bijective morphism
of
(labelled)

transition systems

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
.

The following lemma, analogous to Lemma
4.11
from Section
4.2
, follows from the definition of the
ACD-parity-transform
.

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
n
be a node of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}
, let
\tilde{n}
be an
ancestor
of
n
and let
e=v\xrightarrow{}v^{\prime}
be an edge in
\ell_{i}
. Then,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(n,e)
is a
descendant
of
\tilde{n}
if and only if
e\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(\tilde{n})
, and in this case, if
e_{n}=(v,n)\xrightarrow{}(v^{\prime},n^{\prime})
is an edge of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
, then
n^{\prime}
is a
descendant
of
\tilde{n}
too.

Proof 5.21

(Proof of Proposition
5.19
)
.

We consider the mapping
\varphi=(\varphi_{V},\varphi_{E})
naturally defined by
\varphi_{V}(v,n)=v
and
\varphi_{E}(e_{n})=e
.
It is immediate to check that
\varphi
is a
weak morphism of transition systems
(it preserves
initial states
and transitions). Also, it is easy to see that it is
locally bijective
: for each
initial state

v_{0}\in I
, there is exactly one node in
I^{\prime}
of the form
(v_{0},n)
: the node where
n
is the
leftmost

leaf
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
; and for each vertex
(v,n)
and edge
e\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Out}}(v)
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, we have defined exactly one edge outgoing from
(v,n)
corresponding to
e
.

We prove that
\varphi

preserves the acceptance
of
runs
, following the proof scheme from Proposition
4.10
. We can assume w.l.o.g. (see Remarks
2.1
and
5.17
) that the set of
output colours
used by
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
is its set of edges
E
.
Let
\rho\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Run}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})})}
be an infinite
run
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
. Eventually,
\rho
will remain in one
SCC
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(\rho)
will form a
cycle
that is
accepting
if and only if
\rho
is an
accepting run
.
We will assume that all the edges in
\rho
appear infinitely often and belong to this
cycle
(we can do it by using a similar argument as the one presented in the proof of Proposition
4.10
), and we let:

\rho=(v_{0},n_{0})\xrightarrow{x_{0}}(v_{1},n_{1})\xrightarrow{x_{1}}(v_{2},n_{2})\xrightarrow{x_{3}}\dots.

The projection of
\rho
under
\varphi
is:

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\varphi}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\rho)=v_{0}\xrightarrow{e_{0}}v_{1}\xrightarrow{e_{1}}v_{2}\xrightarrow{e_{3}}\dots.

We note that the edges
\{e_{0},e_{1},\dots\}
form a
cycle
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, that we will call
\ell_{\rho}
. In particular,
\ell_{\rho}
is contained in some maximal
cycle

\ell_{\max}
, and all the nodes
n_{i}
belong to the same tree
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{\max})}
of the
ACD
.
Our objective is to show that
\ell_{\rho}
is an
accepting cycle
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
if and only if
\min\{x_{0},x_{1},x_{2},\dots\}
is even.
We let
\tilde{n}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(n_{i},e_{i})
be the node of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
determining the i
th
transition of
\rho
, so we have that
x_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(\tilde{n}_{i})
.
Finally, let
n_{\rho}
be the
deepest

ancestor
of
n_{0}
such that
\ell_{\rho}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{\rho})
.

Claim 4
.

For all
i\geq 0
,
n_{i}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\succeq}}n_{\rho}
and
\tilde{n}_{i}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\succeq}}n_{\rho}
(that is, all nodes appearing in
\rho
are
below

n_{\rho}
). In particular,
x_{i}\geq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(n_{\rho})
.

{subproof}

The claim follows from Lemma
5.20
and induction.

Claim 5
.

Let
n_{\rho,1},\dots,n_{\rho,s}
be an enumeration of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Children}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{\max})}}(n_{\rho})
. It holds that:

1.

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(n_{i},e_{i})=n_{\rho}
infinitely often. In particular,
x_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(n_{\rho})
for infinitely many
i
’s.

2.

There is no
n_{\rho,k}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Children}}(n_{\rho})
such that
\ell_{\rho}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{\rho,k})
.

{subproof}

The proof is identical to that of Claim
2
, from Proposition
4.10
.

We conclude that
\min\{x_{0},x_{1},x_{2},\dots\}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}(n_{\rho})
, which is even if and only if
\ell_{\rho}
is an
accepting cycle
, by Remarks
5.2
and
5.10
.

.

\rho
