\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\dots,

where the finite runs
\rho_{i}
are
cycles

over
v
, for
i>0
. Therefore, the sequence of these cycles can be processed by the
ZT-parity-automaton
corresponding to the
local Muller condition

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}
. By Lemma
5.9
and the correctness of the
ZT-parity-automaton
, the minimal colour produced by a
run over
this sequence of cycles in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{parity}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}}}}
coincides with the minimal
output colour
produced by the run
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\varphi}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}^{-1}(\rho)
in the
ACD-parity-transform

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
(disregarding the initial path
\rho_{0}
). This colour is exactly the one corresponding to the
deepest
node in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
above the
leftmost

leaf
containing
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(\rho)
.

The
locally bijective morphism
given by Proposition
5.19
witnesses that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
shares the same semantic properties as
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
. The next corollaries follow from Proposition
3.20
and Corollary
3.26
(and the fact that the choice of initial vertices in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
is arbitrary).

Corollary 5.23
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
Muller

automaton
and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}
be its
ACD-parity-transform
. Then,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})})}
, and
\mathcal{A}
is
deterministic
(resp.
history-deterministic
) if and only if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}
is
deterministic
(resp.
history-deterministic
).

Corollary 5.24
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a
Muller

game
and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{G})}
be its
ACD-parity-transform
.
Eve

wins

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{G})}
from a vertex of the form
(v,n)
if and only if she
wins

\mathcal{G}
from
v
.

5.3
An optimal history-deterministic transformation to Rabin transition systems

In this section we describe the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
, an optimal transformation of
Muller

TS
to
Rabin

TS
preserving
history-determinism
. This construction generalises that from Section
4.3
.

Definition 5.25

(ACD-HD-Rabin-transform)
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

TS
. For each vertex
v\in V
we let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}_{v}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})\to\{1,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})}\}
be a mapping satisfying Property (
4.52
) from Lemma
4.52
.

We define the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
to be the
Rabin

TS

\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}=({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}^{\prime})
, with
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}^{\prime}=(V^{\prime},E^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Source}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Target}}^{\prime},I^{\prime})
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime},\allowbreak{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})},\allowbreak{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Rabin}(R)})
defined as follows.

Vertices.

The set of vertices is

V^{\prime}=\bigcup_{v\in V}\left(\{v\}\times\{1,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})}\}\right),

where
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})}
is the
round-branching width
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
.

Initial vertices.

I^{\prime}=\{(v_{0},x)\mid v_{0}\in I\text{ and }x\in\{1,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v_{0}}})}\}\}
.

Edges and output colours.

We let

E^{\prime}=\bigcup_{e\in E}\left(\{e\}\times{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Source}}(e)}})\right).

For each edge
e=v\xrightarrow{}v^{\prime}\in E
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
and
x\in\{1,\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})}\}
, we will place one edge from
(v,x)
for each leaf
l
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}_{v}(l)=x
.
More precisely, we let
(v,x)\xrightarrow{n}(v^{\prime},x^{\prime})\in E^{\prime}
if either

•

v
and
v^{\prime}
are not in the same
SCC
(in this case the output colour
n
is irrelevant), or

•

v
and
v^{\prime}
are in the same
SCC
and there are
leaves

l
and
l^{\prime}
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v^{\prime}}}
, respectively, such that:

–

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}_{v}(l)=x
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\eta}{}{}_{v^{\prime}}(l^{\prime})=x^{\prime}
,

–

l^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Jump}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v^{\prime}}}}(l,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(l,e))
,

–

n={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Supp}}(l,e)
.

Rabin condition.

R=\{(G_{n},R_{n})\}_{n\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}_{\bigcirc}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}}
, where
G_{n}
and
R_{n}
are defined as follows: Let
n
be a
round
node, and let
n^{\prime}
be any node in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}
,

\begin{cases}n^{\prime}\in G_{n}&\text{ if }n^{\prime}=n,\\
n^{\prime}\in R_{n}&\text{ if }n^{\prime}\neq n\text{ and }n\text{ is not an {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}ancestor} of }n^{\prime}.\end{cases}

Labellings.

If
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
is a
labelled transition system
, with labels
l_{V}\colon V\to L_{V}
and
l_{E}\colon E\to L_{E}
, we label
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
by
l_{V^{\prime}}^{\prime}(v,x)=l_{V}(v)
and
l^{\prime}_{E^{\prime}}(e^{\prime})=l_{E}(e)
, if
e^{\prime}\in E^{\prime}(e)
.

This construction generalises the
ZT-HD-Rabin-automaton
in the same way as the
ACD-parity-transform
generalises the
ZT-parity-automaton
. Intuitively, a
run
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
can be identified with a promenade through the nodes of the
ACD
, which are used as the
output colours
to define the
Rabin

acceptance condition
.

Remark 5.26
.

The size of the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
is:

|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}|=\sum_{v\in V}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})}=\sum_{v\in V_{\mathrm{rec}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})}+|V_{\mathrm{trans}}|,

where
V_{\mathrm{rec}}
and
V_{\mathrm{trans}}
are the sets of
recurrent
and
transient
vertices of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, respectively.

Correctness of the ACD-HD-Rabin-transform.

To obtain the correctness of the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
, we follow the same steps as in the proof of the correctness of the
ZT-HD-Rabin-automaton
(Proposition
4.58
).

Proposition 5.27

(Correctness of the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
)
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
(labelled)

Muller

TS
and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
be its
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
. There is an
HD mapping
of
(labelled)

transition systems

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
.

The proof of the next two lemmas are completely analogous to those of Lemmas
4.59
and
4.61
.

Lemma 5.28
.

Let
u=n_{0}n_{1}n_{2}\dots\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}^{\omega}
be an infinite sequence of nodes of the
ACD
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
. The word
u
belongs to
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Rabin}(R)}
, for
R=\{(G_{n},R_{n})\}_{n\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Nodes}_{\bigcirc}(\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}})}}
the Rabin condition of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
, if and only if there is a unique minimal node for the
ancestor relation
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(u)
and this minimal node is
round
.

Lemma 5.29
.

There exists a
morphism of transition systems

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
.

Using these lemmas we can prove Proposition
5.27
.

Proof 5.30

(Proof of Proposition
5.27
)
.

We define the mapping
\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
in the natural way:
\varphi_{V}(v,x)=v
and
\varphi_{E}(e,l)=e
. It is immediate to check that
\varphi
is a
weak morphism
. The fact that
\varphi
preserves
accepting runs
can be proven analogously to the fact that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}^{\mathsf{Rabin}}_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{F}}}})}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Sigma}(\mathcal{F})}
in Proposition
4.58
(by using Lemma
5.28
).

Definition of a
sound resolver
for
\varphi
: In order to show how to simulate runs of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
, we use the fact that we can see
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
as a quotient of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
(Lemma
5.29
). Let
\tilde{\varphi}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be the
locally bijective morphism
given by Proposition
5.19
, and let
\hat{\varphi}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
be the
morphism
given by Lemma
5.29
.
Since
\tilde{\varphi}
is
locally bijective
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\tilde{\varphi}}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}
is a bijection between the runs of the transitions systems
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
and
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, admitting an inverse
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\tilde{\varphi}}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}^{-1}
. Composing this mapping with
\hat{\varphi}
, we obtain a way to simulate the runs from
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\hat{\varphi}}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}\circ{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\tilde{\varphi}}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}^{-1}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Run}^{\infty}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Run}^{\infty}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})})}.

This composition of mappings provides a
sound

resolver
simulating
\varphi
. Formally, let
({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r_{\mathsf{Init}}},r)
be the
resolver
defined as follows. The choice of initial vertices
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r_{\mathsf{Init}}}\colon I\to I^{\prime}
is given by
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r_{\mathsf{Init}}}(v_{0},x)=v_{0}
. The function
r\colon E^{\prime*}\times E\to E^{\prime}
associates to a finite run
\rho\in E^{\prime*}
and
e\in E
the last edge of the run
\hat{\varphi}(\tilde{\varphi}^{-1}(\varphi(\rho)e))
(subscripts have been omitted for legibility). It is easy to check that
({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r_{\mathsf{Init}}},r)
indeed defines a
resolver simulating
\varphi
. Its
soundness
follows from the fact that
\tilde{\varphi}
and
\hat{\varphi}

preserve the acceptance of runs
.

From Proposition
3.20
we obtain:

Corollary 5.31
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
Muller

automaton
and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{A})}
be its
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
. Then,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{A})})}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}
. Moreover,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{A})}
is
history-deterministic
if and only if
\mathcal{A}
is
history-deterministic
.

ACD-HD-Rabin-transform-for-games.

In Section
2.1
, we discussed some technical difficulties appearing when we wanted to define the
composition
of a
game

\mathcal{G}
and an
HD automaton
: as the output of such operation, we would like to obtain a
game
in which
Eve
always chooses the transitions taken in the automaton, even if it is Adam who makes a move in the
game
, which is not the case if
\mathcal{G}
is an arbitrary game. Also, in Section
3.3
we had to introduce
HD-for-games mappings
in order to formalise correct transformations of
games
.
A similar difficulty appears in the context of the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
; we can see the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
of a
game

\mathcal{G}
as a
game
in which, at each moment, first, a move takes place in
\mathcal{G}
, and then a choice is made to update the current node in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{G}}}
. With the current definition of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{G})}
, it is the player who makes the move in the game component who chooses how to update the node in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{G}}}
. This is potentially a problem, as in order to obtain an equivalent game we would like that
Eve
had full control to decide how to update the nodes in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{G}}}
, even when it was Adam who moved in the game component (we note that in Proposition
5.27
we did not claim that there is an
HD-for-games mapping

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
).
In order to obtain a transformation working for games, we need to slightly modify the definition of the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
.

For a
Muller

game

\mathcal{G}

suitable for transformations
, we define its
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform-for-games
, written
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}^{\mathsf{game}}(\mathcal{G})}
. The idea is simply to take from
Adam
the power to update the
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{G}}}
-component of vertices.
The update of this information is delayed of one transition, so it is
Eve
who makes the choice of how to move in the
ACD
.
To do this, we need to introduce some additional vertices controlled by
Eve
.
The formal details of this construction and the proof of correctness can be found in Appendix
B
.

Proposition 5.32

(Correctness of the ACD-HD-Rabin-transform-for-games)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a
Muller

game

suitable for transformations
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}^{\mathsf{game}}(\mathcal{G})}
be its
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform-for-games
. Then, there is an
HD-for-games mapping

\varphi\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}^{\mathsf{game}}(\mathcal{G})}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
.

Corollary 5.33
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a
Muller

game

suitable for transformations
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}^{\mathsf{game}}(\mathcal{G})}
be its
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform-for-games
. Then,
Eve’s

full winning region
in
\mathcal{G}
is the projection of her
full winning region
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}^{\mathsf{game}}(\mathcal{G})}
.

5.4
Optimality of the ACD-transforms

We now state and prove the optimality of both the
ACD-parity-transform
(Theorems
5.34
and
5.35
) and the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform
(Theorem
5.36
).
The proofs of these results will use the optimality of the
automata
based on the
Zielonka tree
(c.f. Section
4
) as a black-box, which will allow us to prove the optimality of both transformations at the same time.
The key idea is that if
\varphi\colon\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
is an
HD mapping
, we can see
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
as an
HD automaton

recognising
the
accepting runs
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
. We can then use
local Muller conditions
at vertices of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
to reduce the problem to
automata

recognising

Muller languages
.

5.4.1
Statement of the optimality results

We state the optimality of the transformations based on the
ACD
. All the results below apply to
labelled transition systems
too. For technical reasons, we need to suppose that all the states of
transition systems
under consideration are
accessible
, an hypothesis that can always be made without loss of generality. We recall that
HD mappings
are in particular
locally bijective morphisms
and
HD-for-games mappings
(c.f. Figure
7
).

Theorem 5.34
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

TS
whose states are
accessible
and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
be a
parity

TS
. If
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
admits an
HD mapping

\varphi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, then, its
acceptance condition
uses at least as many colours as that of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
.

Theorem 5.35
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

TS
whose states are
accessible
and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
be a
parity

TS
. If
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
admits an
HD mapping

\varphi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, then,

|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}|\leq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}|\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}|}
.

Theorem 5.36
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

TS
whose states are
accessible
and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
be a
Rabin

TS
. If
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
admits an
HD mapping

\varphi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, then,

|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}|\leq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}|\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}|}
.

We obtain an analogous optimality result for the
ACD-HD-Rabin-transform-for-games
. In this case, the bound is not tight due to the additional vertices that are added to
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}^{\mathsf{game}}(\mathcal{G})}
(see Appendix
B
for details).

Corollary 5.37
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a
Muller

game

suitable for transformations
whose states are
accessible
and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
be a
Rabin

game
. If
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
admits an
HD-for-games mapping

\varphi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}\to\mathcal{G}
, then,

|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}^{\mathsf{game}}(\mathcal{G})}|\leq 2{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}|\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}|}
.

5.4.2
Discussion: Limits on the applicability of HD automata and preservation of minimality

Before presenting the proofs of the optimality theorems, we discuss some consequences and limitations of our results.

Difficulty of finding succinct history-deterministic automata.

As mentioned in the introduction, several years had to pass after the introduction of
history-deterministic automata

[
44
]
before finding
HD automata
that were actually smaller than equivalent
deterministic
ones
[
57
]
.
As of today, we only know a handful of examples of
\omega
-regular languages
admitting succinct
HD automata

[
1
,
57
,
24
]
, and their applicability in practice has yet to be fully determined. We assert that we can derive from our results some enlightening explanations on the difficulty of finding succinct
HD

parity

automata
, and set some limits in their usefulness in practical scenarios such as LTL synthesis.

First, Corollary
4.15
already sets the impossibility of the existence of small
HD

parity

automata

recognising

Muller languages
. Corollary
5.39
states that if an
HD

parity

automaton

\mathcal{A}
has been obtained as a transformation of a
DMA

\mathcal{B}
, then
\mathcal{A}
is not strictly smaller than a minimal
deterministic

parity

automaton
for
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}
.

Corollary 5.38
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

TS
. A minimal
parity

TS
admitting an
HD mapping
to
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
has the same size than a minimal
parity

TS
admitting a
locally bijective morphism
to
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
.

Corollary 5.39
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
history-deterministic

parity

automaton
. Assume that there exists a
DMA

\mathcal{B}
such that
\mathcal{A}
admits an
HD mapping
to
\mathcal{B}
. Then, there exists a
DPA

\mathcal{A}^{\prime}

recognising

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}|\mathcal{A}^{\prime}|}\leq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}|\mathcal{A}|}
.

Both corollaries follow from an immediate application of Theorem
5.35
.

The ACD-transform does not preserve minimality.

A natural question is whether the
ACD-parity-transform
preserves minimality of automata, that is, given a
DMA

\mathcal{A}
with a minimal number of states for the language it
recognises
, is
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}
minimal amongst
DPAs

recognising

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}
?
11
11

11

This question was left open as a conjecture in the conference version of this paper
[
23
]
.
The answer to this question is negative, as we show now.

Proposition 5.40
.

There exists a
DMA

\mathcal{A}
that is minimal amongst
DMAs

recognising

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A})}
, but such that its
ACD-parity-transform

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}
is not a minimal
DPA
.

We consider the alphabet
\Sigma=\{a,b,c\}
and the language

L=\{w\in\Sigma^{\omega}\mid c\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Inf}}(w)\text{ and }w\text{ contains infinitely often the factor }ab\}.

A minimal
DMA
for
L
is depicted in Figure
18(a)
. Its minimality follows simply from the fact that, as
L
is not a
Muller language
(
(abc)^{\omega}\in L
but
(bac)^{\omega}\notin L
, c.f. Remark
2.10
), a
DMA
with just one state cannot
recognise

L
. In Figure
18
we show its
alternating cycle decomposition
and its
ACD-parity-transform
that has
4
states. However, we can find a
DPA
with just
3
states
recognising

L
, as shown in Figure
18(d)
.

(a)

A
Muller

automaton
with
acceptance set
given by
\mathcal{F}=\{\{\alpha,\beta,\lambda\}\}
.

(b)

Alternating cycle decomposition
of
\mathcal{A}
. To indicate the labels of the nodes of this
ACD
, we include just the colours of the corresponding edges.

(c)

ACD-parity-transform
of
\mathcal{A}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{A})}
, with
4
states.

(d)

A
parity

automaton

recognising

L
with only
3
states .

Figure 18
:
A minimal
DMA
whose
ACD-parity-transform
is not a minimal
DPA
.

5.4.3
Optimality of the parity condition of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}

We show next the proof of Theorem
5.34
. To prove this result, we would like to use the Flower Lemma
2.16
,
however, the statement of Theorem
5.34
does not involve
\omega
-regular languages
. In order to set up a context in which apply the Flower Lemma, we show that, whenever we have a
morphism

\varphi\colon\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
can be seen as an
automaton
reading the
runs
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}=({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}})
and
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}=({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}^{\prime})
be
transition systems
with
underlying graphs

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}=(V,E,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Source}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Target}},I)
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}G}^{\prime}=(V^{\prime},E^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Source}}^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Target}}^{\prime},I^{\prime})
, and
acceptance conditions

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}=(\gamma,\Gamma,\mathbb{W})
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathrm{Acc}}^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},\mathbb{W}^{\prime})
.
A
weak morphism of transition systems

\varphi\colon\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
provides a labelling of the edges of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
by
\varphi_{E}\colon E\to E^{\prime}
. Therefore, we can see
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
as an
automaton
with
input alphabet

E^{\prime}
, inheriting the
underlying graph
and
acceptance condition
from
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
.
We say that this is the
automaton of morphism
\varphi
and denote it by
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
.

We define the
language of accepting runs of
a
transition system

\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
as:

\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{L}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}=\{\rho\in E^{\omega}\mid\rho\text{ is an {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}accepting run} in }\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}\}.

Lemma 5.41
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
and
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
be
transition systems
with a single
initial state
, let
\varphi\colon\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
be a
weak morphism of transition systems
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
be its
automaton
.
Then,
\varphi
is an
HD mapping
if and only if the automaton
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
is
history-deterministic
, and, in this case,

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}})}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime})}.

Proof 5.42
.

We first note that a
resolver
for
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
(in the sense of
HD automata
) is a mapping of the form
r\colon E^{*}\times E^{\prime}\to E
, as
E^{\prime}
is the
input alphabet
of this
automaton
. A
resolver simulating
\varphi
(in the sense of
HD mappings
) is a mapping of the same form.
It is straightforward to check that
(q_{0},r)
is a
sound

resolver
for
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
if and only if
({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r_{\mathsf{Init}}},r)
is a
sound

resolver simulating
\varphi
(where
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r_{\mathsf{Init}}}(q_{0}^{\prime})=q_{0}
is the only possible choice of initial vertex).

We prove that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}})}=\{\rho^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Run}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime})}\mid\rho^{\prime}\text{ is an {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}accepting run}}\}
.
First, we remark that if
\rho
is a
run
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
over
\rho^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Run}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime})}
, then
\rho^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\varphi}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\rho)
, since the
labelling
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
by
input letters
is given exactly by
\varphi
itself. Therefore, if
\rho^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}})}
, there exists an
accepting

run
\rho
over

\rho^{\prime}
, and since
\varphi

preserves accepting runs
,
\rho^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\varphi}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\rho)
is
accepting
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
, proving the inclusion from left to right. For the other inclusion, we let
({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r_{\mathsf{Init}}},r)
be a
sound

resolver simulating

\varphi
. If
\rho^{\prime}
is an
accepting run
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\rho^{\prime})
is an
accepting

run over

\rho^{\prime}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
.

We recall that
[{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}]
are the colours used by the
ACD-parity-transform
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, which coincides with the maximal
height
of a
tree
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
.
We also recall that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}=0
if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
positive
or
equidistant
, and that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}=1
if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
negative
.

Lemma 5.43
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

TS
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell)}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
be a
positive
(resp.
negative
) tree of the
ACD
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
of
height

d
. Then,
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
admits a
positive
(resp.
negative
)
d
-flower
.

Proof 5.44
.

We use the same argument as the one used in the proof of Theorem
4.13
. Let
n_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}}n_{2}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}}\dots\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\preceq}}n_{d}
be a
branch
of length
d
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell)}
(where
n_{1}
is the
root
and
n_{d}
is a
leaf
of the tree). Let
v\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}_{\mathsf{States}}}(n_{d})
be a vertex appearing in the
leaf
. Then, the whole
branch
is contained in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
(by Remark
5.4
), that is,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{i})\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}
. Moreover,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{1})\supsetneq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{2})\supsetneq\dots{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{d})
is a chain that alternates
accepting
and
rejecting

cycles
, so it is a
d
-flower
that is
positive
if and only if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\nu}{}{}(n_{1})=\ell
is an
accepting cycle
, that is, if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell)}
is
positive
.

Lemma 5.45
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be a
Muller

TS
with a single initial vertex and whose vertices are all
accessible
. Then, the
parity index
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
is:

•

[{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}]
if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
positive
or
negative
,

•

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Weak}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}}}
if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
is
equidistant
.

Proof 5.46
.

We consider the identity
morphism

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathit{Id}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}\colon\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
and its automaton
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathit{Id}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}}}
, which is a
deterministic
automaton trivially
recognising

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
(that is, we see
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
as an automaton reading its own edges as
input letters
).
The result follows from the Flower Lemma
2.16
and the fact that a
tree

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell)}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
of
height

d
provides a
d
-flower
that is
positive
if
\ell
is
accepting
and
negative
if
\ell
is
rejecting
(Lemma
5.43
). These
flowers
are
accessible
as we have supposed that all the vertices of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
are
accessible
.

The previous lemmas allow us to obtain Theorem
5.34
for
transition systems
with a single
initial vertex
. We introduce some further notations to deal with the general case.

For a
Muller

TS

\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
and a vertex
v
, we let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{ACD}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}}
be the
alternating cycle decomposition
of the
accessible part of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
from
v
. We note that the
trees
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}}
are a subset of the trees of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
: a
tree

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{AltTree}(\ell_{i})}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
appears in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}}
if and only if the
cycle

\ell_{i}
is
accessible from

v
.
Accordingly, for each vertex
v
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
we let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}{\min}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}}
(resp.
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}{\max}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}}
) be the minimum (resp. maximum) value taken by the function
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}p}_{\mathcal{ACD}}
when restricted to the
trees
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{ACD}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}}
.

Remark 5.47
.

For every
transition system

\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
, one of the two following statements holds:

•

There is some vertex
v
such that
[{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}]=[{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}}]
.

•

There are two vertices
v_{0}
and
v_{1}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v_{0})}}=0
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v_{0})}}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}-1
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v_{1})}}=1
,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v_{1})}}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}
.

Moreover, if all the states of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
are
accessible
, we can choose
v
(resp.
v_{0}
and
v_{1}
) to be an initial vertex.

We can finally deduce Theorem
5.34
from the preceding lemmas.

Proof 5.48

(Proof of Theorem
5.34
)
.

We assume that we are in the first case of Remark
5.47
(a proof for the second case follows easily). First, we show that we can suppose that
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
and
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
have a single initial vertex. Let
v
be an
initial vertex
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
such that
[{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}]=[{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS},v)}}]
.
Let
\varphi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be an
HD mapping
, and let
({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r_{\mathsf{Init}}},r)
be a
sound

resolver
simulating it. We let
\tilde{v}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r_{\mathsf{Init}}}(v)
be the initial vertex in
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
chosen by the resolver. It suffices then to prove the result for the
accessible part of
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
from
\tilde{v}
, the
transition system

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}_{v}}
, and the restriction of
\varphi
to these transition systems.

From now on, we assume that both
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
and
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
have a single initial vertex.
By Lemma
5.45
and Proposition
2.15
, a
parity

history-deterministic automaton
recognising
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
uses at least
|[{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}]|
colours. By Lemma
5.41
, the
automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}

of the morphism

\varphi
is a
parity

history-deterministic automaton
recognising
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}_{\mathpzc{Runs}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
, and therefore uses at least
|[{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\min}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}{\max}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}}]|
colours. Since the
acceptance condition
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}
is exactly the same as that of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{\varphi}}
, we can conclude.

5.4.4
Optimality of the sizes of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{parity}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{ACD}_{\mathsf{Rabin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}

We prove now Theorems
5.35
and
5.36
.

Proof 5.49

(Sketch of the proof)
.

Let
\varphi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
be an
HD mapping
, and let
v
be a vertex in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
. We can see the set
\varphi^{-1}(v)
as the states of an
HD automaton
reading finite
runs
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
looping around
v
. This allows to define an
HD automaton
having
\varphi^{-1}(v)
as set of states and
recognising

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}
. As the
Zielonka tree
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}}(v)}
is the tree
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}}
, by optimality of the
ZT-parity-automaton
(resp. the
ZT-HD-Rabin-automaton
), we deduce that
|\varphi^{-1}(v)|\geq|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Leaves}}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})|
(resp.
|\varphi^{-1}(v)|\geq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{rbw}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{T}_{v}})}
).

Definition 5.50
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
and
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
be two
transition systems
, and let
(\gamma,\Gamma,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{F})})
be the
acceptance condition
of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
.
Let
\varphi\colon\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
be a
weak morphism
of
transition systems
that is
locally surjective
, and let
v^{\prime}
be an
accessible

recurrent
state of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
.
For each
\ell^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime})}
we let
\rho_{\ell^{\prime}}
be a finite
path
starting and ending in
v^{\prime}
visiting exactly the edges of
\ell^{\prime}
.
We define the
cycle-preimage-automaton at
v^{\prime}
to be the
Muller

automaton

\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}}=(Q_{v^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime})},Q_{v^{\prime}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}2^{\Gamma}_{+}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\delta},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}2^{\Gamma}_{+}}}(\tilde{F})})
over the input alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})}
defined as:

•

the set of states is
Q_{v^{\prime}}=\varphi^{-1}(v^{\prime})
,

•

all the states are initial,

•

the
output colours
are non-empty subsets of the colours used by
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
,

•

(q_{2},C)\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\delta}(q_{1},\ell^{\prime})
if there is a finite
path

\rho\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Path}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{q_{1}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
from
q_{1}
to
q_{2}
such that
\varphi(\rho)=\rho_{\ell^{\prime}}
producing as
output
the colours in
C\subseteq\Gamma
, that is
\gamma(\rho)=C
. If
C
is empty, this corresponds to an
uncoloured
edge
q_{1}\xrightarrow{\ell^{\prime}:\varepsilon}q_{2}
.
We remark that, since
\varphi
is assumed
locally surjective
, there is at least one such
path

\rho
.

•

\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}\in\tilde{F}
if and only if
\cup_{i=1}^{k}C_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
.

We remark that a transition
e=q_{1}\xrightarrow{\ell^{\prime}:C}q_{2}
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}}
induces a finite
path

\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathsf{Unfold}}(e)=q_{1}\mathrel{\hbox to11.33pt{\vbox to10.19pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.66576pt\lower-5.64442pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

.

Lemma 5.51
.

If
L={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{\Gamma}(\mathcal{F})}
is a
parity
(resp.
Rabin
) language, then, so is the language
\tilde{L}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Muller}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}2^{\Gamma}_{+}}}(\tilde{F})}
used by the
acceptance condition
of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}}
.

Proof 5.52
.

Assume that
L
is a
parity language
, that is, there are
d_{\min}\leq d_{\max}
and
\phi\colon\Gamma\to[d_{\min},d_{\max}]
such that for any non-empty subset
C\subseteq\Gamma
,
C\in\mathcal{F}
if and only if
\min\phi(C)
is even. We define
\tilde{\phi}\colon{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}2^{\Gamma}_{+}}\to[d_{\min},d_{\max}]
as:
\tilde{\phi}(C)=\min\phi(C)
. It is immediate to see that
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}\in\tilde{F}
if and only if
\min\tilde{\phi}(\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\})
is even.

Assume now that
L
is a
Rabin language
represented by the
Rabin pairs

\{(G_{1},R_{1}),\dots,\allowbreak(G_{r},R_{r})\}
. We define a family of
Rabin pairs

\tilde{R}=\{(\tilde{G}_{1},\tilde{R}_{1}),\dots,(\tilde{G}_{r},\tilde{R}_{r})\}
for
\tilde{L}
as:
\{C_{1},\dots,C_{k}\}\in\tilde{G_{i}}
(resp.
\in\tilde{R_{i}}
) if
\cup_{i=1}^{k}C_{i}\in G_{i}
(resp.
\in\tilde{R_{i}}
). It is immediate to see that
\tilde{L}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textsf{Rabin}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}2^{\Gamma}_{+}}}(\tilde{R})}
.

Lemma 5.53
.

Let
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}
and
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
be two
Muller

TS
,
\varphi\colon\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}\to\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
a
weak morphism of TS
, and
v^{\prime}
an
accessible

recurrent
state of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
.
If
\varphi
is an
HD mapping
, then the
automaton

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}}
is
history-deterministic
and
recognises
the
local Muller condition of
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
at
v^{\prime}
.

Proof 5.54
.

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}})}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{v^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
:
Let
\ell_{1}^{\prime}\ell_{2}^{\prime}\dots\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}^{\omega}
be a sequence of
cycles
accepted by
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}}
. By
prefix-independence
of
Muller languages
we can assume that all the
cycles

\ell_{i}^{\prime}
appear infinitely often.
Let
\rho=q_{0}\xrightarrow{\ell_{1}^{\prime}:C_{1}}q_{1}\xrightarrow{\ell_{2}^{\prime}}q_{2}\xrightarrow{}\dots
be an
accepting run
in
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}}
over
\ell_{1}^{\prime}\ell_{2}^{\prime}\dots
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Unfold}}(\rho)
be its unfolding. As
\rho
is an
accepting run
, so is
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Unfold}}(\rho)
, and since
\varphi

preserves accepting runs
,
\varphi({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Unfold}}(\rho))
is an
accepting run
in
\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}
. The edges visited by
\varphi({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Unfold}}(\rho))
form the
cycle

\cup_{i\geq 1}\ell_{i}^{\prime}
, which is therefore an
accepting cycle
, so
\ell_{1}^{\prime}\ell_{2}^{\prime}\dots\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{v^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS})}
by definition of
local Muller condition
.

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{LocalMuller}_{\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{L}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}})}
and
history-determinism
:
Let
r_{\varphi}:E^{*}\times E^{\prime}\to E
be a
sound

resolver simulating
\varphi
. We will transfer the strategy given by
r_{\varphi}
to define a
resolver

r_{\mathcal{A}}:\Delta^{*}\times{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime})}\to\Delta
for
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}}
, where
\Delta
is the set of transitions of the automaton.
Let
\rho_{0}^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Run}^{\mathsf{fin}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime})}
be a finite run reaching
v^{\prime}
, and let
\rho_{0}={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}r}_{\varphi,\mathpzc{Runs}}(\rho_{0}^{\prime})
the preimage given by the resolver, ending in some
q_{0}\in Q_{v^{\prime}}
that is going to by used as
initial state
for
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{A}_{(\varphi^{-1},v^{\prime})}}
. For a sequence
e_{1}e_{2}\dots e_{k}\in\Delta^{*}
and
\ell^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathpzc{Cycles}_{v^{\prime}}(\mathcal{T\hskip-3.1298ptS}^{\prime})}
, we let

r_{\mathcal{A}}(e_{1}e_{2}\dots e_{k},\ell^{\prime})=r_{\varphi}(\rho_{0}^{\prime}\rho_{1}^{\prime}\dots\rho_{k}^{\prime},\rho_{\ell^{\prime}}),{}

where
\rho_{j}^{\prime}=\varphi({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathsf{Unfold}}(e_{j}))
and
v^{\prime}\mathrel{\hbox to15.45pt{\vbox to10.23pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.72276pt\lower-5.66525pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
